<emptyline>
Length of the rectangle $= w + 2$
<emptyline>

4w                              &= 16 \\\\

w                               &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
w                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {4} \times 4} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
w                               &= 4 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text {Length}                  &= 4 + 2 \\\\
                                &= 6 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area}                    &= 4 \times 6 \\\\
                                &= 24 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of rectangle} = 2 \times (\text{length} + \text{width})$


ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


Let the width of the rectangle $= w$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times (w + 4w)               &= 20 \\\\

10w                             &= 20 \\\\
w                               &= \dfrac {20} {10} \\\\
w                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 2} {10} \\\\
w                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {10} \times 2} {\cancel {10}} \\\\
w                               &= 2 \ \text{cm} \\\\
\text {Length}                  &= 4 \times 2 \\\\
                                &= 8 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Area}                    &= 2 \times 8 \\\\
                                &= 16 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

$16 \ \text {cm}^2$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Another square has an area of $81 \ \text{cm}^2$. 

What is the square's perimeter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


Let the side of the square $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times a          &= 81 \\\\
a^2                 &= 81 \\\\
a                   &= \sqrt {81} \\\\
a                   &= 9 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

Let's find the perimeter of the square.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}       &= 4 \times a \\\\
                        &= 4 \times 9 \\\\
                        &= 36 \ \text{cm}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \ \text{cm}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-2303CD70-1792-44FF-86A3-345BA8D1D16D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

A solid wooden cube is painted blue on the outside.

The cube is then cut into eight smaller cubes of equal size. 

What is the total number of faces of the smaller cubes that have blue paint on them?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Add the diagram with given wooden cube divided into 8 smaller cubes such that each cube has only 3 painted face.*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


Each smaller cube has 3 three face painted with blue.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Total faces}         &= 8 \times 3 \\\\
                            &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A2AA0292-B690-4FB6-B6F2-07B4E9A2598E'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Adrian’s garden measures $10 \ \text{m}$ by $6 \ \text{m}$. 

Paving stones measure $0.5 \ \text{m}$ by $0.5 \ \text{m}$.

How many paving stones does he need to pave his garden?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paving stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the garden}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {0.5 \times 0.5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {0.5 \times 10 \times 0.5 \times 10} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 6 \times 5 \times 2 \times 10} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 2 \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 6 \times 2 \times 10 \\\\
                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$240$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of paving stones} &= \dfrac {\text{Area of the garden}} {\text{Area of a stone}} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6} {0.5 \times 0.5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {10 \times 6 \times 10 \times 10} {0.5 \times 10 \times 0.5 \times 10} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 6 \times 5 \times 2 \times 10} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 2 \times 10} {\cancel {5} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                               &= 2 \times 6 \times 2 \times 10 \\\\
                               &= 240
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$240$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If the figure shown is folded to form a cube, then three faces meet at every vertex (corner).

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


The numbers on the three faces meeting at any vertex can be multiplied together.

What is the largest such product for the vertices of this cube?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $4$ will not come together on a single vertex

Next largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $3$ will come together on a single vertex

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest product} &= 6 \times 5 \times 3 \\\\
                       &= 90
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$90$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $4$ will not come together on a single vertex
<emptyline>
Next largest number combination $6$, $5$, and $3$ will come together on a single vertex
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Largest product} &= 6 \times 5 \times 3 \\\\
                       &= 90
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$90$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A square floor is tiled with square tiles and there are $77$ tiles on the diagonals.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)
 

The diagram shows part of the floor. 

How many tiles are in the room?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the diagram

Let the number of tiles till the middle of diagonal $=a$ 

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of tiles in the diagonal} &= 4 \times a + 1 \\\\
4 \times a + 1                         &= 77 \\\\
4 \times a                             &= 77 - 1 \\\\
4 \times a                             &= 76 \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {76} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                      &= 19
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tiles on the edges} &= 2 \times a + 1 \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 19 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 39
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of tiles}    &= 39 \times 39 \\\\
                                &= 1521 
\end{aligned}
$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1521$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the diagram
<emptyline>
Let the number of tiles till the middle of diagonal $=a$

$

a                                      &= \dfrac {76} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a                                      &= \dfrac {19 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a                                      &= 19
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total tiles on the edges} &= 2 \times a + 1 \\\\
                                &= 2 \times 19 + 1 \\\\
                                &= 39
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$

\text{Total number of tiles}    &= 39 \times 39 \\\\
                                &= 1521 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)

As per the diagram, the shape can be divided into three rectangles.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Perimeter}   &= 3 + 2 + 6 + 2 + 3 + 4 + 12 + 4 \\\\
                    &= 36 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Total Area}  &= \text {Rectangle 1} + \text {Rectangle 2} + \text {Rectangle 3} \\\\
                    &= 3 \times 2 + 3 \times 2 + 12 \times 2 \\\\
                    &= 6 + 6 + 24 \\\\
                    &= 36 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Write down the fraction (in its simplest form) of the area which remains unshaded in each:


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of the area unshaded} &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3 - 1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {2} {3} 
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of the area unshaded} &= 1 - \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1 \times 9} {1 \times 9} - \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {9 - 6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {2} {3}$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$ \dfrac {1} {3}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of the area unshaded} &= 1 - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {1 \times 3} - \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3 - 1} {3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {2} {3} 
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Fraction of the area unshaded} &= 1 - \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1 \times 9} {1 \times 9} - \dfrac {6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {9 - 6} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3} {9} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {3} {3 \times 3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {\cancel 3} {\cancel 3 \times 3} \\\\
                                     &= \dfrac {1} {3}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<div class='question question'>

A school built $6$ tennis courts in the design shown below.
Each court measured $36 \ \text{m}$ by $20 \ \text{m}$.
Between the courts and around the edge, a $1 \ \text{m}$ - wide.
cement path was laid, as shown in the diagram, which is NOT DRAWN TO SCALE.

Calculate the total area **of the paths**.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


*TODO:: Show the diagram with the calculations for the parts of the path which do not have measurements shown.
Show the length of rectangle $43 \ \text {cm}$ and width $112 \ \text {cm}$.*

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


As per the diagram,

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Total area of the path}          &= \text {Total Area} - 6 \times \text {Area of tennis court}  \\\\
                                        &= 43 \times 112 - 6 \times (20 \times 36) \\\\
                                        &= 4816 - 4320 \\\\
                                        &= 496 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$496 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                        &= 496 \ \text {cm}^2
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
